# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-23

The edition has 87 complete guides and 19 screened discoveries. It completes 15 earlier pending guides and details 31 substantive release updates. Today’s research covers editable transcripts and chapters, writable MIDI handoffs, multi-view scene capture and motion transfer. Blender 5.3, released November 10, supports native Gaussian splat import and rendering. Preserve the original splat for handoff because native export is unavailable in that version. XR Blocks v0.25 documents a separate WebGPU route for interactive splat rendering. SOMA 1.0 and QtMeshEditor 3.72 document distinct editable assembly and character capture handoffs. Code grants were read in full. Models, services, creative inputs and hardware or software hosts have separate requirements. This edition is based on source review and published documentation. No discovered app was installed or run. The expanded plan covered 122 fields, 1408/1408 repository-query attempts and 37 model tasks. The repository lane recorded 1406 successful, 2 partial and 0 failed queries, with 257 bounded. The model lane recorded 0 failed tasks. Metadata from 2,887 saved repository pages included 84,375 numeric identities. The complete retained backlog spans 121,834 candidates, including pilot history and 1,070 previously screened guides still pending before this edition. Creative relevance and the complete software grant were checked before including any discovery. Unreviewed and excluded records stay in local research. Live primary research yielded 374 complete usable responses from 460 distinct URL attempts, including a non-GitHub source in each of 122 fields. 4 captures reached the size bound and 82 other sources were inaccessible or unusable. The ecosystem checks cover all 8 configured groups and record available findings and gaps. Evidence labels in this edition are 0 Recommended, 0 Ready to try, 93 Quality unverified, 13 Experimental. Repository searches returned no saved rows for Camera-reactive projection installations, Editable AI transcript chapters and audio publication, Live production accessibility and routed captions, Learned material capture and de-lighting, Learned projector compensation and exhibit color, Neural splat compositing and editor interchange. Primary web research for these fields is included in the coverage notes. Updated evidence labels for previous entries: See2Sound Backend (Quality unverified to Experimental), Cypher DJ (Quality unverified to Experimental), SOMA (Experimental to Quality unverified), OpenGPEX (Quality unverified to Experimental), AtlasForge (Quality unverified to Experimental), OpenFill (Quality unverified to Experimental). 33 model tasks reached the configured response limit of 50 rows. A model record remains a discovery lead until code and use are verified. Model tasks with no rows: text2text-generation, zero-shot-audio-classification, audio-segmentation, image-matting. The separate watchlist made 66 unsuccessful repository checks. Collection logged 5 enrichment failures. Credential-shaped material in unsealed captures was removed before sealing. The affected source origins and before-and-after hashes remain in private evidence.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

| Neural underwater photography, film and scene restoration | UDnet's authors describe neural restoration for green/blue cast and haze. Documented results should be interpreted for the actual water, lighting and camera conditions. [Source 1](https://alzayats.github.io/UDnet/) |
| Learned acoustic holography and instrument field media | The audio proceedings paper was reachable as a primary PDF. The current page capture does not establish maintained creative authoring software, a complete grant or hardware compatibility for deployment. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.09256) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.17626) [Source 3](https://eurasip.org/Proceedings/Eusipco/Eusipco2021/pdfs/0000121.pdf) |
| Learned calligraphy and robotic brush performance | CalliRewrite documents motion planning and manipulation for strokes. Inspect the actual arm control prerequisites and the output at the chosen ink, scale and paper. [Source 1](https://luoprojectpage.github.io/callirewrite/) |
| AI comic localization, relettering and translation | ComicTL describes a custom YOLO-Nano bubble detector and a separate translation step. Match text, lettering and reading order to the source page after the detections. [Source 1](https://comictl.kiuyha.dev/index.html) [Source 2](https://chromewebstore.google.com/detail/hmigninkgibhdckiaphhmbgcghochdjc) |
| AI furniture, timber and joinery design | EPFL’s Augmented Carpentry project documents model-assisted alignment on fabrication tools. Establish the tool setup and the physical reference alignment before assessing the workflow. [Source 1](https://ibois-epfl.github.io/augmented-carpentry/) [Source 2](https://www.epfl.ch/labs/ibois/augmented-carpentry/) |
| AI game localization and playable translations | Taarib's own guide scopes game localization to titles the user can localize. Compare translated text and play-test context separately from licensing the tool. [Source 1](https://abdallah28r.github.io/taarib/en/) |
| AI stage lighting and show control | Ossia and QLC+ document timeline/control options. The camera model's detections and the behavior of a real console or fixture are distinct verification tasks. [Source 1](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/tutorials/inputs/index.html) [Source 2](https://ossia.io/projection-mapping.html) [Source 3](https://lacylights.com/) [Source 4](https://qlcplus.org/) |
| AI photobook, album sequencing and editorial layout | The visual cover study connects generated art to music metadata. It does not verify editable book spreads, pagination or printer-ready attachments. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.01651) |
| AI seamless loops, sample design and DAW-ready stems | WaivePulse describes an offline suite. A captured product page does not establish every underlying model grant or seamless playback at chosen tempos. [Source 1](https://huggingface.co/spaces/ACE-Step/Ace-Step) [Source 2](https://waivepulse.com/) |
| Neural yarn modeling and machine knitting | The inverse procedural yarn study reconstructs parameters from images down to fiber detail. A visual yarn model does not give verified physical fiber composition or production tolerances. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.00154) |
| Neural watermarking for printed creative media | StegaStamp describes hiding information in imagery intended for digital display or printing. Paper, capture and lighting conditions matter when evaluating whether the decoded mark survives. [Source 1](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp) |
| Learned halftones and neural print screening | The archived deep-halftoning research describes a learned reversible binary-pixel arrangement with output examples. Exporting a monochrome image does not establish usable ink density or fidelity on the target press. [Source 1](https://nikronic.com/projects/deep-halftoning_project/) [Source 2](https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/ICCV2021/html/Xia_Deep_Halftoning_With_Reversible_Binary_Pattern_ICCV_2021_paper.html) |
| AI brick sculpture and agent-assisted construction design | BrickGPT and BrickWright report text-directed brick planning and stability evaluation. Instructions or rendered previews do not establish a physical build with the creator's actual part inventory. [Source 1](https://avalovelace1.github.io/BrickGPT/) [Source 2](https://avalovelace1.github.io/brickwright/) |
| Neural optical holography and computational displays | Current lab pages show neural and time-multiplexed holography. Odak provides optics software context. Physical display and camera calibration terms depend on the implementation. [Source 1](https://cinescope-wkr.github.io/pado-hologram/) [Source 2](https://light.princeton.edu/publication/artifact-resilient-holography/) [Source 3](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/neuralholography/) [Source 4](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/time-multiplexed-neural-holography/) [Source 5](https://www.kaanaksit.com/odak/cgh/) [Source 6](https://www.kaanaksit.com/odak/installation/) |
| AI poetry, kinetic literature and language installations | RiTa and JHAVÉ provide procedural writing or generative text context. The application assessment still needs an actual model contribution before entry as an AI tool. [Source 1](https://jhave.com/) [Source 2](https://rednoise.org/rita/) |
| Neural hair, fur and strand-groom authoring | StrandHead, HairGPT and Digital Salon document learned approaches to 3D hair. Strand controls, rig compatibility and safe export depend on the chosen implementation. [Source 1](https://digital-salon.github.io/) [Source 2](https://haiminluo.github.io/hairgpt/) [Source 3](https://xiaokunsun.github.io/StrandHead.github.io/) |
| Neural smoke, fluid capture and editable VFX fields | HyFluid documents a learned scene reconstruction workflow for fluid dynamics. Verify capture and rendering constraints separately from any reusable scene or footage export. [Source 1](https://kovenyu.com/HyFluid/) |
| AI pattern transcription and symbolic craft verification | The StitchFoundry source and model references distinguish an assisted artwork stage from geometrically validated embroidery. Save the layout and review the result before a machine run. [Source 1](https://stitchfoundry.github.io/StitchFoundry/) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.5-4B) |
| Neural rhythm tracking and synchronized visual media | The cited primary research evaluates music-conditioned generation or regularity in movement. Reproducing projections synced to a specific audio and venue remains a separate design task. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.03576) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21658) |
| Foundation-model dioramas and compositional scene assembly | The Diorama authors document single-view scene reconstruction. Hidden surfaces and unobserved dimensions remain uncertain even when the composition looks plausible. [Source 1](https://3dlg-hcvc.github.io/diorama/) |
| AI planner layouts, book arts and binding-aware publishing | Booklet publishes imposition documentation and Rosette documents the publishing workflow. Neither a generated page image nor an OCR layer guarantees readable signatures or ready-to-bind output. [Source 1](https://docs.hornpenguin.com/projects/booklet/en/latest/index.html) [Source 2](https://www.rosette.dev/docs) |
| AI shadow puppetry and hand-shadow performance | First-party CUNY and paper sources describe physical projection and shadow representations. A specific art installation requires checks for actual light geometry, interaction and stage timing. [Source 1](https://academicworks.cuny.edu/ny_pubs/1252/) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10360) |
| AI-agent pixel art, sprites and palette-controlled animation | Gridfab and Pixelbolt document separated authoring routes for grid-based artwork. Palette, raster scale and sprite export need an inspection after any agent edit. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/azrazalea/gridfab) [Source 2](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) |
| Learned robotic shadow performance and inverse silhouette control | Duke's work concerns learned robotic shadow silhouettes. CUNY and Sunway document separate puppet preservation and performance research. None of those contexts by itself clears transferable production code. [Source 1](https://generalroboticslab.com/shadow) [Source 2](https://academicworks.cuny.edu/ny_pubs/1252/) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22434) [Source 4](https://sunwayuniversity.edu.my/research/centres/SCDHCH/woyae-tangae) |
| AI score transcription correction and part layout | Audiveris and current primary score research distinguish automatic music recognition from correction. Check music structure and exact export before typesetting the target edition. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17870) [Source 2](https://audiveris.github.io/audiveris/) |
| Archival film restoration and preservation | Current papers separate synthetic defect creation from reconstructing real film frames. Release and configuration work also showed why training data, learned weights and archival reuse rights need independent review. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02131) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21219) [Source 3](https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2025/html/Mao_Making_Old_Film_Great_Again_Degradation-aware_State_Space_Model_for_CVPR_2025_paper.html) [Source 4](https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/WACV2024/html/Lin_Restoring_Degraded_Old_Films_With_Recursive_Recurrent_Transformer_Networks_WACV_2024_paper.html) [Source 5](https://seungjunnah.github.io/Datasets/reds.html) [Source 6](https://www.archives.gov/preservation/formats) [Source 7](https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/absolutedegradation/absolutecinema-real-scene-split-full) [Source 8](https://www.loc.gov/preservation/digital/formats/fdd/fdd000395.shtml) |
| Camera-reactive projection installations | MediaPipe’s browser Pose Landmarker returns tracked points; TrackOSC describes routing landmarks to a separate controller. Geometry and performer timing require testing on the actual surface. [Source 1](https://ai.google.dev/edge/mediapipe/solutions/vision/pose_landmarker/web_js) [Source 2](https://apps.apple.com/app/trackosc/id6795593815) |
| Learned projector compensation and exhibit color | The SIComp and projector-compensation projects study learned correction for the appearance of a projected image on a real surface. Calibrate the selected camera/projector setup and check changes in ambient light. [Source 1](https://hai-bo-li.github.io/SIComp/) [Source 2](https://bingyaohuang.github.io/projects/projector-compensation/) |
| Spatial audio and sound dramaturgy | AMBIT documents 44.1 kHz FOA generation and editing. PhysWave documents explicit trajectories and short 16 kHz FOA clips. SAID estimates event direction on a panoramic map. Each has a separate model or data review, and the physical or headphone decoder needs its own listening comparison. [Source 1](https://huggingface.co/api/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/api/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/tree/main) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/api/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/runtime) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/raw/main/app.py) [Source 5](https://huggingface.co/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/raw/main/requirements.txt) [Source 6](https://huggingface.co/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/raw/main/README.md) [Source 7](https://huggingface.co/api/models/IN03X/SAID) [Source 8](https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/spaces-zerogpu) |
| Live production accessibility and routed captions | LiveAudio publishes an ONNX Whisper path to OBS for Windows and Linux. LocalVocal provides a separate native filter, including a Mac package matrix. StagePulse documents provider-based stage routing. Validate caption timing in the actual venue or stream. [Source 1](https://liveaudio.opencohost.com/) [Source 2](https://obsproject.com/forum/resources/localvocal-local-live-captions-translation-on-the-go.1769/) [Source 3](https://ossia.io/recording-streaming.html) |
| Cultural heritage layout recognition and transcription | Kraken, eScriptorium and Arkindex document separate recognition and correction workflows for scans and IIIF. Select a model appropriate for the writing and verify the reading order in PAGE or ALTO output. [Source 1](https://escriptorium.eu/about/) [Source 2](https://doc.teklia.com/arkindex_tutorial/export/) |
| Learned optics and post-production lens tools | CalibRaw uses learned masks for targeted photo edits. Caliscope’s learned tracking sits alongside lens calibration and checks a multi-camera rig. Separate these uses from conventional optics calibration and verify the chosen output. [Source 1](https://calibraw.dueckis.de) [Source 2](https://mprib.github.io/caliscope/reconstruction/) [Source 3](https://discorpy.readthedocs.io/) |
| Dialogue replacement and phoneme timing | The Blender Lip Sync listing describes offline speech-driven viseme baking. Montreal Forced Aligner documents phoneme timing when a transcript is available. Review both timing and the animator’s editability before choosing a path. [Source 1](https://extensions.blender.org/add-ons/iocgpoly-lip-sync/) [Source 2](https://montreal-forced-aligner.readthedocs.io/en/latest/) |
| Humming, editable arrangement and stem handoff | PyMSS Studio documents local neural separation with an editable multitrack handoff. Spotify Basic Pitch outputs editable MIDI from an isolated recording. The input assumptions and the resulting artifacts differ. [Source 1](https://pymss.net/en/studio/) [Source 2](https://basicpitch.spotify.com/) |
| Learned material capture and de-lighting | Material Palette describes learned extraction of albedo, normals and roughness from measured image regions. DeepBump documents a separate image-to-normal workflow. The BTF study traces material relightability and leaves release eligibility to a separate source and license review. Direct source gaps in this field affected 1 URL attempts. [Source 1](https://astra-vision.github.io/MaterialPalette/) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20948) |
| Editable AI transcript chapters and audio publication | Narrapy derives M4B navigation from the PDF outline or headings. amagi-listen uses Whisper and long pauses to group timestamps in Markdown. Codeberg's ASR Subtitles writes a separate SRT, and Miragea exports separate transcript and chapter data. Inspect boundaries and speaker annotations before handoff. Direct source gaps in this field affected 13 URL attempts. [Source 1](https://pypi.org/pypi/narrapy/json) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/pypi/amagi-listen/json) [Source 3](https://pypi.org/pypi/miragea-podcast/json) [Source 4](https://codeberg.org/hafnium/ASR_subtitles/raw/branch/main/README.md) |
| Neural splat compositing and editor interchange | Blender 5.3 can bring PLY, SPZ and supported glTF or USD splats into point clouds with editable attributes and native render-engine support. Keep the original splat via a separate route because native export remains absent. Check transforms and high-radiance, low-opacity regions against the source view. Direct source gaps in this field affected 4 URL attempts. [Source 1](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/5.3/rendering/) [Source 2](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/5.3/pipeline_io/) [Source 3](https://code.blender.org/2026/10/splatting-the-gaussians-into-blender/) |

## Search and review counts

122 creative fields; 1408/1408 repository queries attempted; 37/37 model-task queries attempted. 85283 distinct source candidates and 1489 model leads. 87 detailed profiles and 19 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 2 partial repository queries, 257 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Transcription and splatting API searches returned project leads. voisleaf uses a local WhisperX worker and declares AGPL-3.0-or-later with a full grant. Current Retell license and Arkindex release sources were available. Some targeted project reads returned HTTP 429. Other unusable or bounded direct responses in this group totaled 45. [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects?search=transcription&simple=true&per_page=20) [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects?search=splatting&simple=true&per_page=20) [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/glimpsekit%2Fapps%2Fretell/repository/files/LICENSE/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source](https://gitlab.teklia.com/api/v4/projects/arkindex%2Fbackend/releases?per_page=5) [Source](https://gitlab.com/axr28/voisleaf/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/axr28/voisleaf/-/raw/main/LICENSE)
- **codeberg** · searched: Direct repository documentation and full MIT grants were checked for local ASR Subtitles and the Miragea podcast and audiobook clients. The Miragea clients use a separately governed hosted speech API. The two topical Codeberg searches returned HTTP 429. Other unusable or bounded direct responses in this group totaled 4. [Source](https://codeberg.org/miragea-voice/miragea-podcast/raw/branch/main/README.md) [Source](https://codeberg.org/miragea-voice/miragea-podcast/raw/branch/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://codeberg.org/miragea-voice/miragea-audiobook/raw/branch/main/README.md) [Source](https://codeberg.org/miragea-voice/miragea-audiobook/raw/branch/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://codeberg.org/hafnium/ASR_subtitles/raw/branch/main/README.md) [Source](https://codeberg.org/hafnium/ASR_subtitles/raw/branch/main/LICENSE.md)
- **sourcehut** · searched: The SourceHut transcription search returned ELAN file utilities and the renderer search returned conventional projects. Diffuser's primary Cargo manifest declares a local-path dependency, which leaves reproducible installation open. [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=transcription) [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=renderer) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/Cargo.toml)
- **packages** · searched: PyPI returned versioned project metadata for chapter and transcript authoring. Narrapy packages a standalone audiobook CLI and amagi-listen documents timed Markdown output. Registry metadata identifies a package; the full software grant was reviewed separately. Other unusable or bounded direct responses in this group totaled 1. [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/narrapy/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/amagi-listen/json)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: The Blender extension listing documents versioned PLY and SPLAT export paths, distinct from native Blender import and render. OBS LocalVocal remains an example of in-host model-assisted captioning with separate runtime constraints. Other unusable or bounded direct responses in this group totaled 1. [Source](https://extensions.blender.org/add-ons/gv-point-cloud-exporter/versions/) [Source](https://obsproject.com/forum/resources/localvocal-local-live-captions-translation-on-the-go.1769/)
- **project-sites** · searched: OpenDirector publishes an editable video workflow and Field 4 a speech-reactive learned-space patch. Blender developer documentation supplies native splat handoff caveats. Each application has its own runtime and model dependencies. Other unusable or bounded direct responses in this group totaled 1. [Source](https://thinkflowlab.github.io/OpenDirector/en/) [Source](https://openendedgroup.com/field4/audio_ai_speech_embeddings/) [Source](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/5.3/rendering/)
- **research-code** · searched: INRIA's original 3D Gaussian Splatting project describes reconstruction from camera views. The MotionGraphicsBench publication independently describes verifying SVG motion constraints measured across rendered frames. The released benchmark verification code and noncommercial benchmark data have separate terms. [Source](https://repo-sam.inria.fr/fungraph/3d-gaussian-splatting/) [Source](https://huggingface.co/blog/figma-ai/motion-graphics-bench) [Source](https://huggingface.co/datasets/figma-ai/motion-graphics-bench/resolve/main/LICENSE-CODE)
- **international** · searched: The Chinese PyMSS Studio documentation describes a local stem-separation workspace. French Arkindex materials describe structured document workflows. Review export formats and model terms in the selected locale. Other unusable or bounded direct responses in this group totaled 1. [Source](https://pymss.net/zh/studio/) [Source](https://www.teklia.com/arkindex-tutorial)

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. 1. Author a few editable shapes and group them by their intended meaning.
2. Inspect paths and render a preview before committing changes to the selected drawing.
3. Save an SVG master and a PNG preview, then inspect the named groups in Inkscape.

Aoi adds a SteamVR controller overlay driven by a C++ agent supporting model conversation, speech synthesis, selected VR snapshots and system-audio transcription. [Source 1](https://github.com/keybodhi/AoiVR/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/keybodhi/aoiVR/releases/tag/v1.0.0)

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. Scanned PDFs need OCR first. Weak headings can cause fallback sections based on page counts. Inspect the cleaned text, especially tables and multi-column pages.

### How it uses AI

Narrapy uses Kokoro or Piper neural text-to-speech to narrate a text-based PDF and writes chapter navigation into an M4B audiobook. [Source](https://github.com/rakishere/narrapy/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/narrapy/json) [Source](https://huggingface.co/hexgrad/Kokoro-82M)

[Source 1](https://github.com/Open-Cosmic-Software/nyxmix/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://registry.npmjs.org/nyxmix/latest) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/Systran/faster-whisper-small)

### Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://github.com/Open-Cosmic-Software/nyxmix/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://registry.npmjs.org/nyxmix/latest) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/Systran/faster-whisper-small)

1. Use the documented local server route with Node 22 or later and FFmpeg 6 or later.
2. Use the npm package or a source checkout. Keep the server on loopback for the first pass.
3. Add Python 3 and faster-whisper for transcript editing. The default named checkpoint is small.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/Open-Cosmic-Software/nyxmix/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://registry.npmjs.org/nyxmix/latest) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/Systran/faster-whisper-small)

1. Import a short spoken-word clip, then run the transcript action.
2. Compare the proposed transcript cuts with the waveform and listen to each boundary.
3. Export the revised speech and inspect chapter start times and any fades.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/Open-Cosmic-Software/nyxmix/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://registry.npmjs.org/nyxmix/latest) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/Systran/faster-whisper-small)

- **Hardware:** Minimum RAM, VRAM and disk space for the scoped workflow are undocumented.
- **Software:** Node 22+, FFmpeg 6+ with the documented audio codecs and Rubber Band, and Python 3 with faster-whisper for transcription.
- **Platforms:** The UI runs in a modern browser. The maintainer reports Linux server testing and documents a Homebrew route. Server support for other systems remains unverified.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. [Source 1](https://github.com/Open-Cosmic-Software/nyxmix/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Open-Cosmic-Software/nyxmix/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://registry.npmjs.org/nyxmix/latest) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/Systran/faster-whisper-small)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** The full MIT grant includes a separate BSD attribution notice for the bundled RNNoise model. The selected Whisper weights and optional plugin terms are separate.
- **Commercial:** Project code is governed by MIT. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
- **Cost:** The documented local transcription workflow can run without a paid model endpoint. Optional agent backends and third-party plugins have separate terms.

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. ### Limitations

The cleaner and transcript can make awkward cuts around overlap, fillers or music. Review timing manually before export. Keep optional custom plugins disabled in the scoped route. ## Kotonoha

Audio, music & voice · Accessible media & assistive creation · Creative learning & authoring · Editable AI transcript chapters and audio publication

The 0.50 line adds editable timing and optional learned alignment and separation. Version 0.50.1 restores omitted choruses. Version 0.51 optionally uses Claude to order verses while timing follows the voice alignment, with only simulated evaluation for the new route.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Creative use and the code grant are documented. Check-level gaps remain before a recommendation.

Scope: Make a timing reference or language-study artifact from an authorized track, and correct difficult verse placement manually.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-23. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Reviewed the complete project software grant and its redistribution conditions. Model, data and service terms are scoped separately. [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** Version 0.50.1 restores repeated choruses from markers and Whisper-based matching. On the maintainer’s altered-lyric evaluation of 51 songs, alignment within one second rose from 69% to 80% when the chorus appeared once. The 0.51 release reports 90% with simulated perfect line ordering and voice alignment. It explicitly has no measured result from the actual Claude API route. [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/blob/main/AVISOS.md) [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/releases/tag/v0.50.0) [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/releases/tag/v0.50.1)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** The installation and first-use steps are in the cited documentation. Reproduction of this workflow remains unverified. [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/blob/main/AVISOS.md) [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/releases/tag/v0.50.0) [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/releases/tag/v0.50.1)
- **Maintenance and support · documented:** Version 0.51 adds optional model-assisted ordering. The previous 0.50.1 release restores omitted repetitions and preserves some parenthetical backing vocals. [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/releases/tag/v0.51.0)
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use of the exact workflow was not established in this source review.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The application is MIT. Optional models, original recordings and lyrics have separate terms. The documented local aligning path needs device compute and model downloads. Optional configured providers keep their own billing. [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/blob/main/AVISOS.md) [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/releases/tag/v0.50.0) [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/releases/tag/v0.50.1)

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. The 51-song author evaluation used intentionally shortened lyrics. Its higher 0.51 figure uses idealized verse ordering supplied from reference timestamps. Assess the real provider, language and vocal mix separately.

### How it uses AI

The application aligns lyrics to vocal phonemes with narabas and optionally separates voices through WebGPU Demucs. Version 0.51 adds an optional Claude step that orders sung lines while the audio alignment determines their timing. [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/blob/main/AVISOS.md) [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/releases/tag/v0.50.0) [Source](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/releases/tag/v0.50.1)

### Introduction

The application aligns lyrics to vocal phonemes with narabas and optionally separates voices through WebGPU Demucs. Version 0.51 adds an optional Claude step that orders sung lines while the audio alignment determines their timing. [Source 1](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/blob/main/AVISOS.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/releases/tag/v0.50.0) [Source 4](https://github.com/Eliam4510/kotonoha/releases/tag/v0.50.1)

Make a timing reference or language-study artifact from an authorized track, and correct difficult verse placement manually. ### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. - **Weights:** The complete app grant is MIT. The two cited BiRefNet conversion cards declare MIT. Optional models, fonts and input photos have separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Project code is governed by MIT. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. ### How it uses AI

Framewise uses whisper.cpp for captions and a local Qwen model to propose constrained photo-edit operations, with an explicit rules-only fallback when the model is absent. Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. Depth and RGB views contain inferred geometry. The documented output does not establish a ready-to-deliver mesh or exact reconstruction of an unseen side.

### How it uses AI

MVD-Fusion generates correlated RGB and depth views from a single RGB reference by conditioning a diffusion network on projected depth. [Source](https://github.com/zhizdev/mvdfusion/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/zhizdev/mvdfusion/blob/main/ENVIRONMENT.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/zhizdev/mvd-fusion/tree/main) [Source](https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2024/html/Hu_MVD-Fusion_Single-view_3D_via_Depth-consistent_Multi-view_Generation_CVPR_2024_paper.html)

### Introduction

MVD-Fusion generates correlated RGB and depth views from a single RGB reference by conditioning a diffusion network on projected depth. A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/AcTePuKc/Kokoro-Local-Gui/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/hexgrad/Kokoro-82M)

1. Import a short text or EPUB sample into the audiobook table.
2. Assign two distinct voices and preview a single line for each.
3. Render the batch and check joined audio, level changes and pronunciation.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/AcTePuKc/Kokoro-Local-Gui/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/hexgrad/Kokoro-82M)

- **Hardware:** CPU and supported NVIDIA GPU paths are described. Minimum RAM, VRAM and disk are undocumented. Long MP3 merges can temporarily increase memory use.
- **Software:** Python 3.11, uv, PySide6 and PyTorch. The documented optional GPU route uses a compatible NVIDIA CUDA build.
- **Platforms:** Installation is documented for Windows. Other operating-system combinations are undocumented.

CapCap documents faster-whisper transcription, selected translation and local Vietnamese voices for an editable subtitle and dubbing project. [Source 1](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/docs/requirements.md)

### What it is good for

Prepare manually corrected caption cues for a short Vietnamese or bilingual clip in the documented Windows project workflow. [Source 1](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/docs/requirements.md)

### Demo & examples

Current setup and model-resource documentation covers the available audio, subtitle and translation routes. Result reliability for a specific clip remains unverified. [Source 1](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/docs/requirements.md)

### Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/docs/requirements.md)

1. Use the documented Windows 10/11 64-bit installer or Python 3.11 source route.
2. Check the Visual C++ redistributable and included FFmpeg/libmpv runtime.
3. Use Manage Resources to select the intended ASR model and optional translation backend.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/docs/requirements.md)

1. Start with a short local clip and generate speech cues with the selected faster-whisper model.
2. Review timings and speaker text, then use the chosen translation route on a small segment.
3. Preview and export a subtitle draft. Check alignment again in the intended playback software.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/docs/requirements.md)

- **Hardware:** The guide lists NVIDIA GTX 1060 or newer for GPU acceleration, with CPU fallback. Minimum RAM, VRAM and total storage are undocumented.
- **Software:** Windows 64-bit, faster-whisper/CTranslate2, bundled FFmpeg/libmpv and required Visual C++ runtime. CUDA 12.8 for the documented GPU route.
- **Platforms:** Windows 10 and Windows 11 64-bit are documented. Other platforms are undocumented.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. [Source 1](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/docs/requirements.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** App code is Apache-2.0. Whisper, translation, VieNeu-TTS, Piper voices and media carry their own terms.
- **Commercial:** Project code is governed by Apache-2.0. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
- **Cost:** Local transcription can use CPU. The selected translation and voice provider may require a separately governed host or model setup.

### Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [Source 1](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/docs/requirements.md)

### Limitations

Inspected instructions explain several optional browser and hosted translation routes. The scoped workflow uses local captioning first and leaves browser-account automation outside the recommendation. [Source 1](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/notepower2k1/CapCap/blob/main/docs/requirements.md)

## LocalText2Voice

Audio, music & voice · Editable AI transcript chapters and audio publication · Dialogue replacement and phoneme timing · Creative publishing & presentation

The last guide covered v2.2.0. The v3.0.1 release adds Continue generation that verifies existing segment settings and regenerates only missing, failed, changed or invalid audio, preserving matching WAV files across an interrupted job.

Creative use and the code grant are documented. Check-level gaps remain before a recommendation.

Scope: Recover a long narration after an interruption or regenerate a corrected passage while keeping unaffected WAV files and chapter metadata.

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. Continue requires matching segment settings and valid audio. Version 3.0.1 cannot reconstruct old metadata for a replaced reference recording. Manually review subtitles and regenerate affected segments.

### How it uses AI

LocalText2Voice generates narration in editable segments, with optional Whisper word timings and chaptered M4B export. It can preserve compatible segment audio when continuing a project. 1. Open a copy of a short prepared audiobook project and generate a few segments.

### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/estebanstifli/LocalText2Voice/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/estebanstifli/LocalText2Voice/releases/tag/v3.0.1) [Source 3](https://github.com/estebanstifli/LocalText2Voice/blob/main/docs/LINUX.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/estebanstifli/LocalText2Voice/blob/main/docs/INDEXTTS_2_5.md)

- **Hardware:** The author lists 8 GB RAM and 5 GB free disk for basic editing or lightweight generation. NVIDIA 8 GB VRAM is recommended for regular local neural synthesis. Larger model and media projects require more space.
- **Software:** Windows desktop package or Linux source with Python 3.10+, FFmpeg and desktop packages. Individual engines download separate runtimes and weights.
- **Platforms:** Windows 10/11 x64 is the packaged route. Linux x64 source setup is documented for Arch/CachyOS. The maintainer has not documented a supported macOS package.

### How it uses AI

A deformable-convolution model predicts additional light-field views from a sparse angular grid and uses a second network to refine the synthesized field. ZigZag exposes editable embroidery designs to an AI assistant through its MCP connector. The new Agent setup workflow handles selection and connection of the supported clients. [Source](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

ZigZag exposes editable embroidery designs to an AI assistant through its MCP connector. The new Agent setup workflow handles selection and connection of the supported clients. [Source 1](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Ask for a small editable stitch change, inspect jump stitches and stitch order, then verify the intended machine format and physical sample. [Source 1](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

Version 0.1.10 documents in-panel agent setup and retained Jump Stitches and Smart Guides settings. Its README describes the editable embroidery and machine-file workflow. [Source 1](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/README.md)

1. Use the correct macOS, Windows or Linux package from the release.
2. Confirm the supported agent and account route.
3. Keep a separate test project and use the documented machine export for the actual machine.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/README.md)

1. Create a small stitch motif and request one change.
2. Inspect stitch length, color order, jumps and the export preview.
3. Verify the exported design on an appropriate sample before production use.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum RAM, VRAM and disk space for the scoped workflow are undocumented.
- **Software:** Desktop software and optional supported AI client through MCP. Machine export needs the format accepted by the target machine.
- **Platforms:** The current docs offer macOS, Windows and Linux downloads. The macOS guide names macOS 11 or later and Intel and Apple Silicon builds.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. [Source 1](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Complete Apache-2.0 software file with its standard clauses. Source artwork, machine formats, external coding agents and model terms are separate.
- **Commercial:** Project code is governed by Apache-2.0. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
- **Cost:** Verify the selected agent account and any model usage costs, plus the material and machine requirements.

### Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [Source 1](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The release documents a more accessible setup. It does not provide a comparison of finished stitch quality across machines and fabrics. [Source 1](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/README.md)

## OpenCreator

Video, animation & film · AI visual dubbing and translated performance · Dialogue replacement and phoneme timing

Adds transcription checkpoint reuse and segment-aware dubbing recovery for interrupted translation work.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Creative use and the code grant are documented. Check-level gaps remain before a recommendation.

Scope: Use a short authorized multilingual clip, review corrected timing and test recovery of an interrupted localization job using completed segment state.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-23. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Reviewed the complete project software grant and its redistribution conditions. Model, data and service terms are scoped separately. [Source](https://github.com/krillinai/OpenCreator/blob/master/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The v3.3.4 release documents retained transcription checkpoints and segment-aware dubbing recovery. It also documents clearer startup, credit and task errors. [Source](https://github.com/krillinai/OpenCreator/blob/master/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** The installation and first-use steps are in the cited documentation. Reproduction of this workflow remains unverified. [Source](https://github.com/krillinai/OpenCreator/blob/master/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · documented:** v3.3.4 preserves completed transcription and dubbing work where the task route supports resuming. [Source](https://github.com/krillinai/OpenCreator/releases/tag/v3.3.4)
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use of the exact workflow was not established in this source review.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Apache-2.0 software. Voice consent, source footage, model downloads and cloud-provider terms remain separate. Provider credits and local inference runtime depend on the configured engines. Recovery is designed to reuse completed work. [Source](https://github.com/krillinai/OpenCreator/blob/master/README.md)

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. Maintainer recovery fixes apply to named task checkpoints. Accent performance, synchronization and behavior with different providers need separate evaluation.

### How it uses AI

OpenCreator combines transcription, language-model translation and dubbing, with task records that can resume from already completed transcription or dubbing segments. [Source](https://github.com/krillinai/OpenCreator/blob/master/README.md)

### Introduction

OpenCreator combines transcription, language-model translation and dubbing, with task records that can resume from already completed transcription or dubbing segments. [Source 1](https://github.com/krillinai/OpenCreator/blob/master/README.md)

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. This creates known training views. It does not perform the training by itself. Inspect scene scale, trainer export, lighting and transparency at handoff.

### How it uses AI

The Blender add-on exports rendered views, known camera poses and a start point cloud for a Gaussian splat trainer. It also documents using source-mesh depth to remove point artifacts from the trained scene. Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. Refitted source motions and uncertainty data are derived from existing motion capture. Use the correct force-independent pose input for force evaluation. An animation or plugin production export is outside this evidence.

### How it uses AI

ForceBody publishes MIT-licensed loaders and evaluation code for learned force-aware body motion, with joint torque and uncertainty paired to parametric movement. [Source](https://github.com/DominoAI-Lab/ForceBody-NeurIPS-ED-2026/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22769) [Source](https://huggingface.co/datasets/kwonjoon/ForceBody) [Source](https://skel.is.tue.mpg.de/)

### Introduction

ForceBody publishes MIT-licensed loaders and evaluation code for learned force-aware body motion, with joint torque and uncertainty paired to parametric movement. [Source 1](https://github.com/DominoAI-Lab/ForceBody-NeurIPS-ED-2026/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22769) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/datasets/kwonjoon/ForceBody) [Source 4](https://skel.is.tue.mpg.de/)

### What it is good for

Review how contact forces and uncertain joints behave in a small reference-motion sequence for research-oriented animation. [Source 1](https://github.com/DominoAI-Lab/ForceBody-NeurIPS-ED-2026/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22769) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/datasets/kwonjoon/ForceBody) [Source 4](https://skel.is.tue.mpg.de/)

### Demo & examples

The authors publish sample walking, stair and jump visualizations with force arrows and torque uncertainty bands. These describe the released benchmark scope. [Source 1](https://github.com/DominoAI-Lab/ForceBody-NeurIPS-ED-2026/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22769) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/datasets/kwonjoon/ForceBody) [Source 4](https://skel.is.tue.mpg.de/)

### Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://github.com/DominoAI-Lab/ForceBody-NeurIPS-ED-2026/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22769) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/datasets/kwonjoon/ForceBody) [Source 4](https://skel.is.tue.mpg.de/)

1. Use the project code and a Python/NumPy environment for reading trial arrays.
2. For rendering, follow the separate Python 3.9–3.12 visualization dependency list.
3. Review the external dataset and the separately gated SKEL model terms before obtaining any required files.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/DominoAI-Lab/ForceBody-NeurIPS-ED-2026/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22769) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/datasets/kwonjoon/ForceBody) [Source 4](https://skel.is.tue.mpg.de/)

1. Choose a small rights-appropriate example and read the matching trial manifest.
2. Apply frame-validity masks and choose the pose source relevant to movement generation or force estimation.
3. Inspect contact vectors and temporal uncertainty before using the sequence as reference.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/DominoAI-Lab/ForceBody-NeurIPS-ED-2026/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22769) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/datasets/kwonjoon/ForceBody) [Source 4](https://skel.is.tue.mpg.de/)

- **Hardware:** Numeric RAM, VRAM and storage for the complete visualization workflow are undocumented.
- **Software:** Python, NumPy for data alone, with PyTorch, pyrender and the licensed SKEL model for the renderer.
- **Platforms:** The publication specifies Python versions and renderer options. A tested platform and GPU matrix is undocumented.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. [Source 1](https://github.com/DominoAI-Lab/ForceBody-NeurIPS-ED-2026/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/DominoAI-Lab/ForceBody-NeurIPS-ED-2026/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22769) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/datasets/kwonjoon/ForceBody) [Source 5](https://skel.is.tue.mpg.de/)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** The repository code is MIT, benchmark data CC BY-NC 4.0 and required SKEL model noncommercial. Scope the combined research workflow accordingly.
- **Commercial:** Project code is governed by MIT. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
- **Cost:** Local data processing. Obtaining the separate model requires access conditions specified by its publisher.

### Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [Source 1](https://github.com/DominoAI-Lab/ForceBody-NeurIPS-ED-2026/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22769) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/datasets/kwonjoon/ForceBody) [Source 4](https://skel.is.tue.mpg.de/)

### Limitations

Refitted source motions and uncertainty data are derived from existing motion capture. Use the correct force-independent pose input for force evaluation. An animation or plugin production export is outside this evidence. [Source 1](https://github.com/DominoAI-Lab/ForceBody-NeurIPS-ED-2026/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22769) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/datasets/kwonjoon/ForceBody) [Source 4](https://skel.is.tue.mpg.de/)

## Hudka

Audio, music & voice · AI Foley and sound effects · Video, animation & film · AI seamless loops, sample design and DAW-ready stems

First complete guide in the library. The publication and documentation dates are not necessarily the project’s launch date.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Creative use and the code grant are documented. Check-level gaps remain before a recommendation.

Scope: Build a draft sound-effects track for an owned short video, revise individual cue timing and verify the license report before handoff.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-23. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Reviewed the complete project software grant and its redistribution conditions. Model, data and service terms are scoped separately. [Source](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The maintainer publishes a cue-sheet workflow and an RTX 4070 timing estimate for a 48-second clip. The documented test scope is Windows/NVIDIA. [Source](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/LICENSING.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-audio-3-small-sfx)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** The installation and first-use steps are in the cited documentation. Reproduction of this workflow remains unverified. [Source](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/LICENSING.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-audio-3-small-sfx)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** The reviewed sources do not establish workflow-specific support, fixes and unresolved blockers.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use of the exact workflow was not established in this source review.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The complete Hudka code grant is MIT. The selected gated model terms and input/voice/media rights are separate. Review the scope of the generated license report before use. The selected local model route uses storage and device compute. The optional contact-sheet analysis sends frames to a configured hosted model and can incur charges. [Source](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/LICENSING.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-audio-3-small-sfx)

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. Automatic cues need human review and use alternate source clips only with permission. The two documented alternate engines are explicitly untested.

### How it uses AI

Hudka combines scene/motion analysis with local learned sound and music generation, then mixes timed cues alongside original dialogue. The editable cue sheet can be designed locally or with an optional model that reviews contact sheets. [Source](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/LICENSING.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-audio-3-small-sfx)

### Introduction

Hudka combines scene/motion analysis with local learned sound and music generation, then mixes timed cues alongside original dialogue. The editable cue sheet can be designed locally or with an optional model that reviews contact sheets. [Source 1](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/LICENSING.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-audio-3-small-sfx)

### What it is good for

Build a draft sound-effects track for an owned short video, revise individual cue timing and verify the license report before handoff. [Source 1](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/LICENSING.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-audio-3-small-sfx)

### Demo & examples

The maintainer publishes a cue-sheet workflow and an RTX 4070 timing estimate for a 48-second clip. The documented test scope is Windows/NVIDIA. [Source 1](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/LICENSING.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-audio-3-small-sfx)

### Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/LICENSING.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-audio-3-small-sfx)

1. Review the named Windows source and setup path and current FFmpeg and uv requirements.
2. Read the selected gated Stability Audio model card and terms before obtaining the weights.
3. Verify the chosen host runtime and storage path, then begin with a short permitted clip.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/LICENSING.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-audio-3-small-sfx)

1. Generate or author a small editable cue sheet using the local route.
2. Render several candidate effects and check placement and loudness against important original speech.
3. Make a limited cue revision, rerender, and retain the original source, cue file and license report.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/LICENSING.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-audio-3-small-sfx)

- **Hardware:** The author estimates about 5 GB working storage and reports 1.7 GB peak VRAM on an RTX 4070 test. CPU mode is documented with different processing time.
- **Software:** The source documents a Windows setup with Python managed by uv, FFmpeg/PySceneDetect and separately acquired audio models.
- **Platforms:** The diagnosed desktop path covers Windows. The author says Linux/macOS need adaptations for bundle launch and drive detection.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. [Source 1](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/LICENSING.md) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-audio-3-small-sfx)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** The complete Hudka code grant is MIT. The selected gated model terms and input/voice/media rights are separate. Review the scope of the generated license report before use.
- **Commercial:** Project code is governed by MIT. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
- **Cost:** The selected local model route uses storage and device compute. The optional contact-sheet analysis sends frames to a configured hosted model and can incur charges.

### Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [Source 1](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/LICENSING.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-audio-3-small-sfx)

### Limitations

Automatic cues need human review and use alternate source clips only with permission. The two documented alternate engines are explicitly untested. [Source 1](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/preetamnegi7/hudka/blob/main/LICENSING.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-audio-3-small-sfx)
